Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Hi, Dr. Smith. Um, well, what brings me in is that I just feel like I can’t settle in my own body anymore. It's really bad especially when I’m supposed to sit quietly and handle paperwork or, you know, shared household plans with my husband. We were actually trying to sort out our taxes last week, and he got this new software that's supposed to make it easier, but honestly, the interface is just so clunky. I don't understand why they change these things every year. It's like the grocery store, right? As soon as you know where the peanut butter is, they move it to aisle seven. Anyway, I just... I can't sit still for it anymore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, looking back, I think this was already part of me in elementary school. I mean, I was always the girl with the messy desk and forgotten permission slips. Oh, and the notes from teachers saying I talked out of turn or just couldn't stay seated. Actually, I remember my third-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable. She had this huge collection of ceramic frogs on her desk. Hundreds of them. Every time someone went on vacation, they'd bring her back a frog. My family went to Florida that year, and my mom made me pick out a frog for her from this little gift shop near the beach. It was bright green with little sunglasses. But honestly, I don't know where she kept them all when she retired. I mean, what do you do with that many frogs? But yeah, the teachers always said I was a handful.

manifestation: Waiting in line at the pharmacy or sitting until it’s my turn to speak in a committee meeting makes me tense and fidgety, and I catch myself checking the time over and over.\n- key: S018\n  name: Interrupting_Intruding\n  description: Often interrupts or intrudes on others (e.g., butts into conversations, games, or activities).\n  manifestation: I care a lot about being respectful, but I still jump into my husband’s conversations or finish people’s thoughts because the response comes out before I’ve made myself wait.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: permission slips, and notes from teachers saying I talked out of turn or seemed unable to stay seated.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: The fallout has been that people at home and in my volunteer circles can’t reliably count on me, and I’ve had to step back from some responsibilities because I keep letting things fall through despite trying hard.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 1 year and 5 months\n  manifestation: It has been noticeably worse since I got married and our routines merged, about a year and five months ago.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 41 / not_in_workforce / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S012\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hi, welcome in. I'm Dr. Smith. To start, can you tell me what brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi, Dr. Smith. Um, well, what brings me in is that I just feel like I can’t settle in my own body anymore. It's really bad especially when I’m supposed to sit quietly and handle paperwork or, you know, shared household plans with my husband. We were actually trying to sort out our taxes last week, and he got this new software that's supposed to make it easier, but honestly, the interface is just so clunky. I don't understand why they change these things every year. It's like the grocery store, right? As soon as you know where the peanut butter is, they move it to aisle seven. Anyway, I just... I can't sit still for it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed this kind of restlessness and trouble focusing since you were a child, or is this something that started more recently in your adult life?\n\nPatient: Well, looking back, I think this was already part of me in elementary school. I mean, I was always the girl with the messy desk and forgotten permission slips. Oh, and the notes from teachers saying I talked out of turn or just couldn't stay seated. Actually, I remember my third-grade teacher, Mrs. Gable. She had this huge collection of ceramic frogs on her desk. Hundreds of them. Every time someone went on vacation, they'd bring her back a frog. My family went to Florida that year, and my mom made me pick out a frog for her from this little gift shop near the beach. It was bright green with little sunglasses. But honestly, I don't know where she kept them all when she retired. I mean, what do you do with that many frogs? But yeah, the teachers always said I was a handful.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a distinct period—lasting several days or more—where you felt unusually elevated, euphoric, or irritable, along with much more energy than usual, that was different from how you normally feel?\n\nPatient: Elevated and euphoric? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, I never really have days and days of just endless energy where I feel totally different like that. I kind of wish I did, honestly! Like last week, I was looking at the side yard and thinking I really need to pull the weeds, but I just couldn't get the motivation together. \n\nMy neighbor, Brenda, though—she is always out there. She has this immaculate rose garden. I really don't know how she does it. She was telling me about this special fertilizer she uses, it's made from kelp or fish emulsion or something? It smells absolutely awful, to be honest. I was standing by the fence talking to her on Tuesday and the smell just hit me. But her roses are huge. They're like those prize-winning ones you see at the state fair. Have you ever been to the state fair here? The fried dough is amazing, but the parking is a total nightmare. We went three years ago and had to park almost a mile away in this muddy field, and I completely ruined my favorite pair of sneakers... wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, energy. But yeah, no, I don't get those super high periods.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you find yourself worrying excessively about a lot of different things—like health, finances, or family—even when there's no clear reason to, and does that worry feel hard to control?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Worrying excessively? Um, I mean, I worry about normal things, I guess. Like, I wouldn't say I sit around agonizing over every little thing for no reason. I do worry about money sometimes, especially with the grocery prices lately. Have you seen how much eggs are? It's ridiculous. I used to bake a lot, you know, just for fun. Muffins, cookies, that sort of thing. My husband loves my oatmeal raisin cookies. But honestly, who puts raisins in cookies when chocolate chips exist? It's like a betrayal when you bite into one thinking it's chocolate and it's a raisin. I actually bought this huge bag of chocolate chips at Costco last month, thinking I'd do a big baking day. But then the mixer started making this weird grinding noise. It's a KitchenAid, and they're supposed to last forever, right? My mom has had hers since the eighties. Hers is this really pretty buttercup yellow color. They don't make that color anymore, which is a shame, because everything now is stainless steel or black, and it just looks so industrial. But anyway, what was the question? Oh, worrying. No, I don't think I worry more than the average person.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, it really does. I mean, the fallout has been pretty bad lately. It’s like people at home and even in my volunteer circles just can’t reliably count on me anymore. I actually had to step back from some of the responsibilities I had for the PTA because I just keep letting things fall through, no matter how hard I try to keep it all straight. 

I was supposed to organize the silent auction for the spring carnival this year. We usually get local businesses to donate gift baskets and gift cards. Last year, this one bakery donated a dozen of these incredible gourmet cupcakes. They were huge, with that really rich buttercream frosting. The woman who runs the bakery, Sarah, she actually used to be a corporate lawyer before she quit to bake full-time. Isn't that wild? Just dropping a whole career to make cupcakes. I don't think I could ever do that. I mean, I have a degree in communications, but I haven't used it in years. I always thought about going back to work, maybe doing something in PR, but then the kids came along, and, you know, life just happens. My youngest, Leo, he's obsessed with dinosaurs right now. He has this toy T-Rex that roars so loudly, it drives the dog absolutely crazy. The dog—Buster, he's a golden retriever mix—he just hides under the couch whenever Leo brings it out. The dust bunnies under there, though... I really need to vacuum. I bought one of those robot vacuums, but it keeps getting stuck on the fringe of the rug in the living room. It just beeps helplessly until someone rescues it. 

Wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the auction. Yeah, I just couldn't keep track of the donation forms and missed the deadline to print the bid sheets, so I had to hand it over to someone else. It feels awful, honestly, because I really do want to help, but I just can't seem to hold it all together.

Oh, I hate that I do this. I really care a lot about being respectful, you know? My parents were huge on manners. But I still jump into my husband’s conversations constantly, or I just finish people’s thoughts for them. It’s like the response just comes out before I’ve made myself wait. 

Like just the other day, my husband was telling our neighbor about his new grill—he got one of those pellet smokers? He is completely obsessed. He spent six hours smoking a brisket on Sunday. Six hours! And honestly, it was a little dry. I didn't tell him that, obviously. I made a really good potato salad to go with it, though. I use red potatoes and leave the skins on, it adds so much texture. And a little bit of fresh dill. Never the dried kind in the little glass jars, it just tastes like dust. Spices are so expensive now, aren't they? I went to buy pure vanilla extract last week and it was almost twenty dollars for a tiny bottle! It’s because of some vanilla bean shortage in Madagascar, I read an article about it. Or maybe it was a podcast? I listen to a lot of podcasts when I'm folding laundry. There's this one about... wait, sorry, what were you asking me? Oh, interrupting and blurting things out. Right. Yeah, I just can't seem to stop myself from jumping in, even though I really don't mean to be rude.

Subtypes: Active Avoidance, Functional Impairment due to Avoidance, Enduring with Distress, Reliance on Safety Signals\n[S065] Obsessions: Recurrent and persistent thoughts, urges, or images that are experienced as intrusive and unwanted, and attempts to ignore or suppress them.  Subtypes: Contamination Obsessions, Pathological Doubt, Symmetry and Exactness Obsessions, Forbidden or Taboo Thoughts, Somatic Obsessions\n[S066] Compulsions: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to an obsession or to prevent anxiety/distress; clearly excessive or not realistically connected.  Subtypes: Cleaning or Washing Compulsions, Checking Compulsions, Ordering and Arranging Compulsions, Mental Acts, Repeating Rituals\n[S067] Preoccupation_with_Appearance: Preoccupation with one or more perceived defects or flaws in physical appearance that are not observable or appear slight to others.  Subtypes: Skin Defects, Facial Features, Asymmetry, Muscle Dysmorphia, Specific Body Parts\n[S068] Appearance_Related_Repetitive_Behaviors: Repetitive behaviors or mental acts performed in response to appearance concerns.  Subtypes: Mirror Checking or Avoidance, Excessive Camouflaging, Skin Picking, Reassurance Seeking, Comparing, Seeking Cosmetic Procedures\n[S069] Intrusive_Symptoms: Recurrent, involuntary, and intrusive distressing memories of the traumatic event. Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight. Subtypes: Persistent Fear, Persistent Horror, Persistent Anger, Persistent Guilt, Persistent Shame\n[S075] Social_Detachment: Feelings of detachment or estrangement from others. Subtypes: Depersonalization, Derealization, Time Distortion\n\n=== PATIENT UTTERANCE ===\nWorrying excessively? Um, I mean, I worry about normal things, I guess. It's ridiculous. I used to bake a lot, you know, just for fun. Muffins, cookies, that sort of thing. My husband loves my oatmeal raisin cookies. But honestly, who puts raisins in cookies when chocolate chips exist? It's like a betrayal when you bite into one thinking it's chocolate and it's a raisin. I actually bought this huge bag of chocolate chips at Costco last month, thinking I'd do a big baking day. But then the mixer started making this weird grinding noise. It's a KitchenAid, and they're supposed to last forever, right? My mom has had hers since the eighties. Hers is this really pretty buttercup yellow color. They don't make that color anymore, which is a shame, because everything now is stainless steel or black, and it just looks so industrial. But anyway, what was the question? Oh, worrying. No, I don't think I worry more than the average person.\n\nTask:\n- CONFIRMED: symptoms clearly present or strongly implied by the patient's words.\n- DENIED: symptoms the patient explicitly says they do NOT have\n  (e.g. \"No\", \"I don't\", \"I haven't\", \"never\").\n- Do NOT speculate. Err toward UNKNOWN (omit) rather than guessing.\n\nReply ONLY with valid JSON (no markdown, no extra text):\n{\n  \"confirmed\": [\"S001\", ...],\n  \"denied\": [\"S025\", ...],\n  \"reasoning\": {\n    \"S001\": \"one-sentence justification\",\n    ...\n  }\n}"
  }

Subtypes: Self-Induced Vomiting, Misuse of Medications\n[S049] Eating_Large_Amount: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n[S050] Loss_of_Control: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n[S051] Rapid_Eating: Eating much more rapidly than normal.\n[S052] Eating_Until_Uncomfortably_Full: Eating until feeling uncomfortably full.\n[S053] Eating_Without_Hunger: Eating large amounts of food when not feeling physically hungry.\n[S054] Eating_Alone_Due_to_Embarrassment: Eating alone because of feeling embarrassed by how much one is eating.\n[S055] Negative_Affect_After_Binge: Feeling disgusted with oneself, depressed, or very guilty afterward.\n[S084] Low_Weight_Severity_Non_Recognition: Persistent lack of recognition of the seriousness of the current low body weight.  Subtypes: Denial of Medical Risk, Disregard of Symptoms, Dismissal of Concerns\n[S056] Excessive_Anxiety_Worry: Excessive anxiety and worry (expectant apprehension) occurring persistently about a number of events or activities.  Subtypes: Chronic Worry, Generalized Worry, Catastrophizing, Intolerance of Uncertainty\n[S057] Uncontrollable_Worry: The subjective experience of finding it difficult to stop or control the worry.  Subtypes: Failure to Control, Intrusive Worry, Chaining of Worry\n[S058] Restlessness_Tension: Restlessness, or feeling keyed up or on edge.  Subtypes: Feeling Keyed Up, Physical Tension, Nervousness\n[S059] Concentration_Difficulty: Difficulty concentrating or mind going blank.  Subtypes: Interference by Worry, Inability to Sustain Attention, Mind Going Blank\n[S060] Irritability: A state of reacting sensitively or sharply to minor stimuli.  Subtypes: Low Frustration Tolerance, Snappiness, Exhausted Patience\n[S061] Muscle_Tension: A state where muscles are continuously tense, stiff, and accompanied by discomfort.  Subtypes: Neck and Shoulder Tension, Jaw Tension, Generalized Muscle Aches, Chronic Stiffness\n[S062] Specific_Phobic_Fear: Marked fear or anxiety about a specific object or situation that is out of proportion to the actual danger. Subtypes: Anticipatory Anxiety, Immediate Panic Response, Physiological Panic Symptoms, Freezing\n[S064] Phobic_Avoidance_Endure: The phobic object or situation is actively avoided or endured with intense fear or anxiety.
